**Definition (learn):** Japanese workers and farmers who tried to organise into a political party were ruthlessly suppressed by police; the war boom ended in 1921; the 1929 crisis shrank exports (✅ Lowe via owner 04 §8.6A).
**Breakdown:**
- Suppression of farmers and industrial workers' political party attempt (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
- Rice prices collapsed after the war boom ended in 1921 (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
- 1929 crisis: exports shrink disastrously; other countries raised tariffs (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
- Raw silk fell by 1932 to less than one-fifth of its 1923 price (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
- Chain to military control at the start of the 1930s (✅ owner 04 §9; cross-link basic/12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1921; 1929; 1932; raw silk below one-fifth of 1923 price.
**Links:** WH-1150, WH-1154, WH-1153.
**Trap:** Do not praise Japanese industrialisation without naming the 1930s outcome (✅ owner 04 §8.6A verdict).
**Mains hook:** Labour denied legal routes turned into militarism rather than labour politics (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Back:** *"Twenty-one, twenty-nine, thirty-two: the silk falls, the army rises."*

### Flashcard WH-1152 — Japan's military-industrial programme: China (1894–95) and Russia (1904–05)
**Front:** Why were industrial and military capacity the same project in Japan?
**Definition (learn):** Korea and then Manchuria were colonised, leading to wars with China (1894–95) and Russia (1904–05); Japan won both (✅ Lowe via owner 04 §8.6A).
**Breakdown:**
- Defeat of Russia: "the first time that an Asian country had defeated one of the European great powers" (✅ Lowe via owner 04 §8.6A).
- By 1918 Japan had a powerful navy and a well-trained, well-equipped army (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
- Locked Japan into a territorial logic that ended in the 1930s crisis (✅ owner 04 §8.6A limitation).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1894–95; 1904–05; 1918.
**Links:** WH-1146, WH-1151, WH-1155.
**Trap:** Do not separate military and industrial capacity in Japan (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Mains hook:** Latecomer industrialisation changed the balance of power.
**Back:** *"Ninety-four China, oh-five Russia — industry and army as one project."*

### Flashcard WH-1153 — Japan's First World War boom (cotton exports trebled, fleet doubled)
**Front:** How did the First World War accelerate Japanese industrialisation?
**Definition (learn):** Japan supplied the Allies with shipping and goods and filled Asian orders Europeans could not; cotton-cloth exports "almost trebled" and the merchant fleet "doubled in tonnage" (✅ Lowe via owner 04 §8.6A).
**Breakdown:**
- External shock as accelerator; a latecomer grows fastest when incumbents are occupied (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A).
- Vulnerability created by the boom's end in 1921 (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** First World War; exports "almost trebled"; fleet "doubled in tonnage" (✅ owner 04 §8.9 bans beyond these).
**Links:** WH-1147, WH-1151, WH-1154.
**Trap:** Do not invent output or tonnage figures beyond these.
**Mains hook:** Incumbent distraction as a transferable latecomer point.
**Back:** *"War boom: cloth trebled, fleet doubled."*

### Flashcard WH-1154 — Japan's export dependence and vulnerability
**Front:** What is the strongest single limitation of Japan's compressed, export-led path?
**Definition (learn):** Compressed industrialisation buys speed at the price of exposure to export markets (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Breakdown:**
- War boom lasted only until mid-1921 (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
- 1929 crisis; tariffs against Japanese goods (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
- Raw silk below one-fifth of 1923 price by 1932 (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
- Britain's comparison: dependence on export markets and imported raw cotton (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1921; 1929; 1932.
**Links:** WH-1151, WH-1153, WH-1156.
**Trap:** Do not present vulnerability as absent in Britain; both were exposed (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Mains hook:** Speed bought at the price of exposure.
**Back:** *"Fast growth, fragile exports."*

### Flashcard WH-1155 — Perry (1853) to Meiji (1868): motive versus capacity
**Front:** What is the named evidence chain for Japan's industrialisation start?
**Definition (learn):** Perry's 1853 treaties to the Meiji restoration in 1868 (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Breakdown:**
- Significance: industrialisation began as a response to a demonstrated military-technological gap (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A).
- Limitation: humiliation explains the motive, not capacity; centralisation supplied capacity (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A).
- 1853 opening; 1868 restoration (✅ owner 04 §8.9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1853; 1868.
**Links:** WH-1145, WH-1146, WH-1148.
**Trap:** Do not date Japan's effective start to 1853; 1868 is the state-action start (⚠️ owner 04 §8.2).
**Mains hook:** Motive from humiliation; capacity from centralisation.
**Back:** *"Fifty-three shock, sixty-eight state."*

### Flashcard WH-1156 — Verdicts: why Japan could industrialise, and was state direction superior
**Front:** What are the owner's verdicts on the Japanese latecomer route?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 04 §8.6A verdict scaffolds (⚠️ analytic).
**Breakdown:**
- Why Japan could: converted a security threat into a state programme; imported technology; built railways, roads and textiles; created institutions; financed and protected change.
- Compare: Britain's industrialisation unplanned; Japan's an instrument of policy; Britain's costs answered by labour law and franchise; Japan's by police suppression and militarism.
- Was state direction superior? "Faster, not superior" — fifty years instead of a century, but with export dependence and militarised political economy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Fifty years vs a century (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A rhetorical).
**Links:** WH-1145, WH-1151, WH-1152, WH-1154.
**Trap:** Do not praise Japanese industrialisation without its ending.
**Mains hook:** Use "faster, not superior" as a graded verdict.
**Back:** *"Faster, not better — and it ended in the army."*

### Flashcard WH-1157 — Japan's latecomer thesis
**Front:** What is the qualified latecomer thesis for Japan?
**Definition (learn):** Japan industrialised as latecomer by treating industry as an instrument of national security and by centralising the state first (⚠️ owner 04 §8.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Security framing (WH-1146).
- Centralisation first: "For the first time in over two and a half centuries Japan became a unified and centralised empire" (✅ Lowe via owner 04 §8.6A).
- Effective start date 1868, not 1853 (⚠️ owner 04 §8.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1868; 1853.
**Links:** WH-1146, WH-1148, WH-1155.
**Trap:** Do not present 1853 as the start of Japanese state industry.
**Mains hook:** The latecomer thesis answers "why Japan but not others?"
**Back:** *"Security first, centralise, then build."*

### Flashcard WH-1158 — Russia as a controlled third case: state-led industrialisation
**Front:** How does Russia serve as a controlled third case in the comparison?
**Definition (learn):** State-led industrialisation is a family of cases, not a Japanese peculiarity; its common risk is social strain outrunning political adaptation (✅ owner 04 §8.6A; ✅ owner 13 cross-reference via owner 04 §9).
**Breakdown:**
- Later, state-assisted industrial path (✅ owner 04 §8.6).
- Strategic pressure, agrarian question unresolved (✅ owner 04 §8.6A referencing basic/13 §9.4).
- Use only in one sentence; do not build a third column (✅ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None in this file.
**Links:** WH-1144, WH-1156 (Japan latecomer verdict); a Russia card belongs to owner 13.
**Trap:** Do not import Russian industrial figures from this file.
**Mains hook:** One sentence showing the family of state-led cases.
**Back:** *"State-led growth: a family, not an exception."*

### Flashcard WH-1159 — Social effects of industrialisation: women, children, city, state, protest
**Front:** Which social dimensions should an "effect on society" answer cover?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 04 §8.7 evidence bank (✅ and ⚠️ tags as in the owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Wage workers: insecure livelihood (✅ owner 04 §8.7).
- Women and children: household economy restructured (✅ owner 04 §8.7; ⚠️ not a single trajectory).
- City: urban public health, housing and sanitation become state problems (⚠️ owner 04 §8.7).
- Collective response: union repeal 1824; Chartism 1830s–40s; the social question becomes constitutional (⚠️ owner 04 §8.7).
- State: 1802 Factory Act; laissez-faire eroded (✅ owner 04 §8.7).
- Environment: qualitative (⚠️ owner 04 §8.7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1802, 1824, 1830s–40s.
**Links:** WH-1132, WH-1133, WH-1134, WH-1135, WH-1136, WH-1137.
**Trap:** Do not give a single trajectory for women's labour.
**Mains hook:** Structure the social-consequences section in these six lines.
**Back:** *"Wage, women, city, union, state, smoke."*

### Flashcard WH-1160 — Industrial Revolution factual-risk controls
**Front:** Which factual claims about the Industrial Revolution must be avoided?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 04 §8.9 controls (✅ owner 04 §8.9).
**Breakdown:**
- Do not attribute the steam engine's invention solely to Watt.
- Do not date to a single year; use c. 1750 (old NCERT) or c. 1760s–1840s.
- No output, GDP, wage or population figures for Britain.
- Spread was uneven: France and Belgium after 1815; Germany later; USA especially after 1870; Russia and Japan later.
- Do not import Indian deindustrialisation statistics.
- Cotton gin: Whitney, US, 1793.
- Japanese figures only as sourced.
- Dates: 1853, 1868, 1889, 1890, 1894–95, 1904–05 only.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** WH-1113, WH-1126, WH-1140, WH-1145, WH-1148, WH-1152.
**Trap:** Owner-vs-Wikipedia start date difference (c. 1750 vs c. 1760) to be flagged.
**Mains hook:** Precision of dates is part of the answer's credibility.
**Back:** *"No single year, no British statistics, no Indian figures here."*

### Flashcard WH-1161 — Japanese and British comparison: the verdict sentence for exam writing
**Front:** Compare the British and Japanese paths in one qualified sentence.
**Definition (learn):** Owner 04 §8.6A verdict (⚠️ analytic).
**Breakdown:**
- Both began in textiles and both ended in factory society.
- Britain's industrialisation: unplanned outcome of private advantage.
- Japan's: instrument of state policy.
- Britain's social costs answered by labour law and franchise; Japan's by police suppression and militarism.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond earlier cards.
**Links:** WH-1145, WH-1151, WH-1156.
**Trap:** Do not write two separate narratives (owner 04 §8.1).
**Mains hook:** Use the comparison grid, not two narratives.
**Back:** *"Same textiles, different author, different costs."*

### Flashcard WH-1162 — PYQ routes into the Industrial Revolution owner (2023 railways; 2024 handicrafts)
**Front:** Which PYQ demands route to owner 04, and how are they handled?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 04 §9 PYQ control: the 2023 GS-I railway question is a direct World History route; the 2024 GS-I England-and-Indian-handicrafts demand is bridged here while the India verdict is Modern History-owned (✅ owner 04 §9).
**Breakdown:**
- 2023 GS-I railways: differentiated effects by country type (WH-1141 to WH-1144) (✅ owner 04 §9).
- 2024 GS-I handicrafts: English mechanism bridged (WH-1164); India verdict in Modern Indian History (✅ owner 04 §8 header, §9).
- Japanese latecomer comparison: a recorded recurring pre-2018 demand; no verbatim 2013 text held (✅ owner 04 §8.6A provenance note).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023; 2024.
**Links:** WH-1140, WH-1141, WH-1164.
**Trap:** Do not invent question numbers or wording not in the ledger (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Mains hook:** Route the railway question through country-type differentiation.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three railways, twenty-four handicrafts."*

### Flashcard WH-1163 — Mark-scaled structures for the Industrial Revolution
**Front:** How should 10, 15 and 20-mark answers on the Industrial Revolution be structured?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 04 §8.3 (⚠️ analytic).
**Breakdown:**
- 10 marks: thesis → two mechanisms (e.g. mechanisation and factory discipline) → one social consequence → one qualification → verdict.
- 15 marks: thesis → 4–5 conditions → mechanism chain → social consequences (class, gender, city) → state and labour response → qualification (unevenness) → verdict.
- 20 marks: all of the above plus diffusion beyond Britain or global/imperial dimension, environmental and demographic effects, historiographical caution, verdict.
- Comparison directive routes: §8.6 for same technology in different economies; §8.6A for different routes into industrialisation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1115, WH-1131, WH-1139, WH-1145, WH-1159.
**Trap:** Choose the correct bank before writing (owner 04 §8.3).
**Mains hook:** The bank choice is part of the directive fidelity.
**Back:** *"Ten, fifteen, twenty — and pick the right bank."*

### Flashcard WH-1164 — The English-to-handicrafts hand-off sentence
**Front:** What one-sentence hand-off links the English mechanism to Indian handicrafts?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 04 §8.5 hand-off sentence (⚠️ analytic, bounded).
**Breakdown:**
- English mechanisation, steam and cheap transport made machine-made cloth structurally cheaper than hand production (✅ owner 04 §8.5 steps 1–4).
- Whether, how far and where that translated into decline of Indian handicrafts depends on colonial policy, patronage and demand inside India (✅ owner 04 §8.5).
- Caution: cheaper competing supply is a pressure, not automatic outcome; decline uneven by craft, region and market segment (⚠️ owner 04 §8.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1140, WH-1162.
**Trap:** "The Industrial Revolution destroyed Indian handicrafts" is a directive failure (✅ owner 04 §8.5).
**Mains hook:** Hand off to Modern Indian History owner after the sentence.
**Back:** *"Cheaper cloth is the push; India's policy decides the fall."*

### Flashcard WH-1165 — Living standards debate: mixed blessing or social catastrophe?
**Front:** What is the optimist versus pessimist debate on the Industrial Revolution, and how should it be handled?
**Definition (learn):** Optimists stress rising output and cheaper goods; pessimists stress long hours, slums, insecurity and child labour; the best handling separates short-run misery from long-run productivity (⚠️ owner 04 adv §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Living standards: output rose, goods cheaper, long-run prosperity expanded vs early workers faced long hours, slums, insecurity and child labour (⚠️ owner 04 adv §2).
- State versus market: enterprise and innovation vs unregulated capitalism producing exploitation; labour laws and the modern state grew (⚠️ owner 04 adv §2).
- Speed: "revolution" captures visible rupture; some sectors changed gradually and unevenly (⚠️ owner 04 adv §2).
- Old NCERT ends by calling industrialisation a source of both benefits and hardships or evils (✅ owner 04 adv §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None numeric (owner 04 adv gives no figures).
**Links:** WH-1138 (traps), WH-1139 (mixed-blessing verdict), WH-1159 (social effects), WH-1134 (Factory Act).
**Trap:** Do not claim the Industrial Revolution improved all social groups equally; gains were uneven and conflict-ridden (✅ owner 04 adv §6).
**Mains hook:** "The Industrial Revolution was a mixed blessing." Critically examine: concede the short-run cost, then argue the long-run productivity gain and the state response.
**Back:** *"Short-run misery, long-run productivity, and the law that followed."*

### Flashcard WH-1166 — Industrialisation and imperialism: intensification, not mechanical causation
**Front:** How does industrialisation relate to imperial expansion?
**Definition (learn):** Industry did not mechanically create empire but greatly intensified the pressure for it (⚠️ owner 04 adv §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Chain: rising industrial output → need for raw materials, markets and investment outlets → competition among industrial powers → colonial domination and imperial rivalry (✅ owner 04 adv §4 diagram, Old NCERT).
- Industrial countries subordinated the economies of weaker regions to their own interests (✅ old NCERT via owner 04 adv §4).
- Industrial capitalism links tightly to imperial rivalry in the late 19th century (⚠️ owner 04 adv §1).
- Do not explain imperialism by racism or nationalism alone; industrial economic demand is central (✅ owner 04 adv §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Late 19th century (⚠️ owner 04 adv §1).
**Links:** WH-1139 (industry and empire verdict), WH-1162 (PYQ routes), WH-1140 (boundary to India owner), Topic 07 (New Imperialism, owned elsewhere).
**Trap:** "Industry caused empire" is wrong; "industry intensified the stakes of empire" is the qualified formulation (✅ owner 04 §8.1).
**Mains hook:** Analyse the relationship between industrial capitalism and imperial expansion: a mechanism of pressure, not automatic causation.
**Back:** *"Output needs markets; rivals take them; empire is the result."*

### Flashcard WH-1167 — Uneven spread of industrialisation by country
**Front:** Which countries industrialised when and in what manner?
**Definition (learn):** Old NCERT cues for the uneven spread across regions (✅ owner 04 adv §5).
**Breakdown:**
- England: earliest and most advantageous start; benchmark case (✅).
- France, Belgium, Switzerland: industrial growth after 1815; continental catch-up (✅).
- Germany: later but powerful rise; importance of national integration (✅).
- USA: rapid growth after independence and especially after 1870; resource-rich industrial power (✅).
- Russia and Japan: later and state-assisted paths; modernisation uneven and compressed (✅).
- Spread was uneven, not even across Europe (✅ owner 04 §8.9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** After 1815 (continent); after 1870 (USA).
**Links:** WH-1141 to WH-1144 (railways by country type), WH-1145 (Britain vs Japan), WH-1158 (Russia as third case).
**Trap:** Do not claim the Industrial Revolution spread evenly across Europe (✅ owner 04 §8.9).
**Mains hook:** The uneven spread is the reason to compare country types instead of writing one narrative.
**Back:** *"England first, Europe after 1815, USA after 1870, Russia and Japan by the state."*

### Flashcard WH-1168 — Protection versus free trade: late industrialisers used tariffs
**Front:** Did industrialisation automatically mean free trade everywhere?
**Definition (learn):** No; industrialisation strengthened the pressure for tariffs, market protection and imperial competition, and many late-industrialising states used protection (✅ owner 04 adv §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Laissez-faire is associated with early capitalist belief in minimum state interference (✅ owner 04 adv §6).
- Industrialisation strengthened pressure for tariff policy and market protection (✅ owner 04 adv §6).
- Many late industrialisers used protection (✅ owner 04 adv §6 trap).
- Japan's "Japan industrialised as an instrument of state policy" fits the protection pattern (⚠️ owner 04 §8.6A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None numeric.
**Links:** WH-1134 (Factory Act), WH-1145 (Britain vs Japan), WH-1019 (capitalism doctrine, Part 1), WH-1025 (capitalism forms).
**Trap:** ❌ industrialisation automatically meant free trade everywhere → ✅ many states protected their industries.
**Mains hook:** Use protection as the reason state direction, not market, shaped latecomer industrialisation.
**Back:** *"Free trade was the early English creed, not the universal rule."*

### Flashcard WH-1169 — 2024 GS-I PYQ: England and the decline of Indian handicrafts (English-side mechanism)
**Front:** What is the verbatim 2024 GS-I question and what English-side mechanism does this owner supply?
**Definition (learn):** "How far was the Industrial Revolution in England responsible for the decline of handicrafts and cottage industries in India? (Answer in 250 words)" (✅ verbatim, owner 04 adv §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanised factory production with lower unit costs → larger output and demand for raw materials and overseas markets → British manufactured imports gain a structural advantage (✅ owner 04 adv §7).
- The "how far" assessment belongs to Modern Indian History advanced owner 07, which adds colonial tariffs and monopoly, lost court patronage, transport and regional variation (⚠️ owner 04 adv §7).
- Do not answer the India-specific causal question from World History alone (✅ owner 04 adv §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024; 250 words.
**Links:** WH-1164 (hand-off sentence), WH-1140 (boundary), WH-1162 (PYQ routes).
**Trap:** "Decline was total" or "Britain alone destroyed handicrafts" is a directive failure on "how far" (✅ owner 04 §8.5).
**Mains hook:** Use the English mechanism for the first half, then the Indian verdict from the Modern Indian History owner.
**Back:** *"Cheaper machine cloth is the English push; India decides how far."*

### Flashcard WH-1170 — 2023 GS-I PYQ: socio-economic effects of railways in different countries
**Front:** What is the verified 2023 GS-I railway demand and its directive?
**Definition (learn):** "Socio-economic effects of railways in different countries" is the 2023 routed demand, marked as "Bring out", 10 marks, 150 words (✅ owner 04 adv §9 PYQ integration).
**Breakdown:**
- Directive: Bring out; 10 marks; 150 words (✅ owner 04 adv §9).
- Route: differentiated effects by country type — Britain, continental Europe, USA, Russia and Japan, colonies (✅ owner 04 §8.6).
- Owner requirement: context, core dimensions, evidence and examples, counterpoint and concise conclusion (✅ owner 04 adv §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023; 10 marks; 150 words.
**Links:** WH-1141 to WH-1144 (railway cards), WH-1162 (PYQ control), WH-1163 (mark structures).
**Trap:** Do not write one undifferentiated paragraph on railways (✅ owner 04 §8.6).
**Mains hook:** A 150-word answer needs three country types and one counterpoint.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three rails: one technology, several outcomes."*

### GROUP — Congress of Vienna and Concert of Europe (owner 05)

### Flashcard WH-1171 — Vienna settlement and Concert of Europe as the conservative restoration
**Front:** What was the Congress of Vienna and what did it try to achieve?
**Definition (learn):** The 1814–15 settlement that reorganised Europe after Napoleon as the conservative response to the French Revolution; the Concert of Europe was the great-power consultation practice that followed (✅ NCERT and Britannica via owner 05 §2, §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Congress met 1814–15; Final Act signed June 1815 (✅ owner 05 §1; ✅ Wikipedia gives 9 June 1815).
- Three principles: legitimacy, balance of power and compensation (✅ owner 05 §2; ⚠️ the fetched Wikipedia text does not list three principles, so keep the owner's formulation flagged).
- Aim: prevent another continent-wide revolutionary upheaval, not only punish France (⚠️ owner 05 §2).
- Concert of Europe: post-Napoleonic great-power consensus favouring preservation of the territorial and political status quo (✅ Britannica via owner 05 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1814–15 (Congress); June 1815 (Final Act; Wikipedia 9 June 1815).
**Links:** WH-1172 (ladder), WH-1174 (principles), WH-1185 (Concert), WH-1197 (verdict), WH-1017 (conservatism doctrine, Part 1).
**Trap:** Vienna did not create nation-states by popular will; it largely restored dynasties (✅ owner 05 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Vienna succeeded at preventing a continent-wide war and failed at legitimacy" — the dual verdict.
**Back:** *"Restore dynasties, balance powers, pay the winners, talk in congresses."*

### Flashcard WH-1172 — Vienna-to-1848 chronology ladder
**Front:** What are the dated stages from Vienna to the 1848 revolutions?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 05 §1 ladder of diplomatic, repressive and adaptive stages (✅ owner 05 §1).
**Breakdown:**
- 1814–15 — Congress of Vienna (✅).
- June 1815 — Final Act (✅; Wikipedia 9 June 1815).
- 1815 — German Confederation of 39 states; Holy Alliance formed (✅).
- 1818 — Congress of Aix-la-Chapelle; France readmitted (✅).
- 1819 — Carlsbad Decrees (✅).
- 1820–21 — Troppau and Laibach congresses (✅).
- 1822 — Congress of Verona (✅).
- 1830 — Belgian independence accepted (✅).
- 1848 — revolutions across Europe (✅).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** WH-1171, WH-1179, WH-1184, WH-1188 to WH-1193.
**Trap:** Keep the date control: each stage is separate (✅ owner 05 §9).
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to show deferral and the 1830/1848 test.
**Back:** *"Fifteen Vienna, eighteen Aix, nineteen Carlsbad, twenty-two Verona, thirty Belgium, forty-eight revolt."*

### Flashcard WH-1173 — Metternich: dominant but not sole controller
**Front:** Who was Metternich and how much did he control?
**Definition (learn):** Metternich of Austria was the dominant statesman at Vienna and influential in Central Europe, but did not control all Europe (✅ owner 05 §2; ⚠️ owner 05 adv §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Dominant Austrian statesman at Vienna (✅ owner 05 §2).
- Shaped Germany and Italy through influence and intervention (✅ owner 05 adv §3).
- Britain and Russia had their own priorities (⚠️ owner 05 adv §6).
- No verbatim quotation attributed to him (✅ owner 05 §8 control).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1179, WH-1189, WH-1197.
**Trap:** "Metternich alone controlled all Europe" is a close-option error (✅ owner 05 adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Use Metternich as the Central-European centre of conservatism, not the entire system.
**Back:** *"Central Europe's chief, not Europe's master."*

### Flashcard WH-1174 — The three principles: legitimacy, balance of power, compensation
**Front:** What three principles governed the Vienna settlement?
**Definition (learn):** Legitimacy, balance of power and compensation (✅ owner 05 §2; ✅ owner 05 §7.4 evidence bank).
**Breakdown:**
- Legitimacy: dynastic right as the test of a valid state (✅ owner 05 §7.4).
- Balance of power: buffer states and France contained rather than destroyed (✅ owner 05 §7.4).
- Compensation: territory redistributed to the victors (✅ owner 05 §7.4).
- Each has one territorial illustration (WH-1175 to WH-1177).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Three principles (✅ owner 05).
**Links:** WH-1175, WH-1176, WH-1177, WH-1171.
**Trap:** The fetched Wikipedia text did not enumerate three principles (⚠️ owner-only enumerated list).
**Mains hook:** Principle → territorial application → consequence is the answer's spine.
**Back:** *"Legit, balanced, compensated."*

### Flashcard WH-1175 — Legitimacy: Bourbon restoration and dynastic rulers
**Front:** How did the legitimacy principle show on the map?
**Definition (learn):** Bourbon monarchy restored in France; dynastic rulers restored in several parts of Europe (✅ owner 05 §2, §7.4).
**Breakdown:**
- Bourbon restoration in France (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Dynastic right made the test of a valid state — the claim 1848 attacked (⚠️ owner 05 §7.4).
- France was contained rather than destroyed (✅ owner 05 §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** WH-1174, WH-1193.
**Trap:** Legitimacy meant dynastic, not national legitimacy.
**Mains hook:** Legitimacy created the test 1848 later attacked.
**Back:** *"Dynasty, not nation, made a state valid."*

### Flashcard WH-1176 — Balance of power: buffers and Swiss neutrality
**Front:** Which buffer arrangements and neutrality served the balance of power?
**Definition (learn):** Belgium and Holland joined into the Kingdom of the Netherlands as a buffer; Switzerland's neutrality recognised (✅ owner 05 §3, §7.4).
**Breakdown:**
- Netherlands buffer against France (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Switzerland's neutrality recognised as a long-term diplomatic principle (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Durable great-power equilibrium, and an artificial Dutch-Belgian union that broke in 1830 (⚠️ owner 05 §7.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1830 (Belgian separation).
**Links:** WH-1180, WH-1192, WH-1174.
**Trap:** Belgium's separation in 1830 is not in 1815 (✅ Wikipedia: Belgium separated 1830).
**Mains hook:** Buffers designed against France created a union that could not hold.
**Back:** *"A buffer that broke: Netherlands, 1830 Belgium."*

### Flashcard WH-1177 — Compensation: who gained territory in 1815
**Front:** Which powers were rewarded with territory at Vienna?
**Definition (learn):** Compensation redistributed territory to the victors (✅ owner 05 §3, §7.4).
**Breakdown:**
- Prussia gained Rhineland, Westphalia and part of Saxony (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Austria received Lombardy and Venetia (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Sardinia-Piedmont strengthened by Genoa (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Most of the Duchy of Warsaw passed to Russia as Congress Poland (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Rewarded victors with populations that had not consented (⚠️ owner 05 §7.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None numeric; do not give population or area figures (✅ owner 05 §8).
**Links:** WH-1178, WH-1181, WH-1182, WH-1187.
**Trap:** Do not give population or area figures for any transfer.
**Mains hook:** Compensation as the seed of later unification.
**Back:** *"Prussia, Austria, Piedmont and Russia are paid."*

### Flashcard WH-1178 — Vienna armed its own gravediggers (Prussia and Piedmont)
**Front:** Why does the compensation principle carry an irony for later unification?
**Definition (learn):** The compensations created Prussia and Sardinia-Piedmont, which later destroyed the settlement's central European arrangements (⚠️ owner 05 §7.4).
**Breakdown:**
- Prussia's Rhineland gain strengthened it for German leadership (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Piedmont's strengthening through Genoa mattered for Italian unification (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Both later powered unification against Vienna's own design (⚠️ owner 05 §7.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1859–71 (unifications, cross-ref Topic 06).
**Links:** WH-1177, WH-1179, WH-1181, WH-1197.
**Trap:** Do not say the settlement "drew all borders permanently" (✅ owner 05 §7.8).
**Mains hook:** The "gravediggers" irony is a high-value 15/20-mark line.
**Back:** *"Prussia and Piedmont, paid to hold, used to break."*

### Flashcard WH-1179 — German Confederation: 39 states under Austria
**Front:** What was the German Confederation of 1815?
**Definition (learn):** A confederation of 39 states under Austrian influence, which postponed national unity (✅ owner 05 §1, §3; ✅ Wikipedia: 39 states led by the Austrian Emperor).
**Breakdown:**
- Created in 1815 (✅ owner 05 §1).
- 39 states (✅ owner 05 §5; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Formed from roughly 300 former Holy Roman Empire states (✅ Wikipedia).
- National unity postponed, not solved (✅ owner 05 §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 39 states; 1815 (✅).
**Links:** WH-1180, WH-1189, WH-1222 (Frankfurt, owner 06).
**Trap:** Do not give any number other than 39 for the German Confederation (✅ owner 05 §7.8).
**Mains hook:** German unity postponed by the Confederation.
**Back:** *"Thirty-nine states, Austria in the chair."*

### Flashcard WH-1180 — United Kingdom of the Netherlands (Belgium joined and then separated)
**Front:** What was the Netherlands arrangement at Vienna and how did it end?
**Definition (learn):** Belgium and Holland were joined as the Kingdom of the Netherlands; Belgium became separate in 1830 (✅ owner 05 §3; ✅ Wikipedia "United Kingdom of the Netherlands" 1830 separation).
**Breakdown:**
- Buffer against France (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Joined under House of Orange-Nassau (✅ Wikipedia).
- Belgium absorbed without consent, reversed in 1830 (✅ owner 05 §7.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1830.
**Links:** WH-1176, WH-1192.
**Trap:** Belgium's independence is 1830 — not a Vienna provision (✅ owner 05 §5).
**Mains hook:** Use Belgium as the one case where the Concert accepted secession.
**Back:** *"Joined in fifteen, split in thirty."*

### Flashcard WH-1181 — Italy under Austrian influence
**Front:** What did Vienna give Austria in Italy, and why did it breed resentment?
**Definition (learn):** Austria received Lombardy and Venetia and influenced much of the peninsula (✅ owner 05 §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Lombardy and Venetia to Austria (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Influence over much of the peninsula (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Source of later Italian resentment (✅ owner 05 §3, §7.6).
- Italy kept divided (✅ owner 05 adv §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** WH-1177, WH-1178, Topic 06.
**Trap:** Do not say Italy was unified in 1815 — it was divided.
**Mains hook:** Austrian Italy as the grievance behind 1848 and 1859–70.
**Back:** *"Lombardy and Venetia to Vienna; Italy stays divided."*

### Flashcard WH-1182 — Poland as Congress Poland under Russia
**Front:** How did Vienna treat Poland?
**Definition (learn):** Most of the Duchy of Warsaw passed under Russian control as Congress Poland (✅ owner 05 §3).
**Breakdown:**
- National aspirations subordinated to great-power bargaining (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Clearest case of a nation subordinated (✅ owner 05 §7.6).
- Partitioned arrangement confirmed (✅ owner 05 §7.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None numeric.
**Links:** WH-1177, WH-1200.
**Trap:** Congress Poland is not an independent state.
**Mains hook:** The Polish case shows Vienna's indifference to national claims.
**Back:** *"Poland goes to Russia as Congress Poland."*

### Flashcard WH-1183 — Switzerland's recognised neutrality
**Front:** What did Vienna settle for Switzerland?
**Definition (learn):** Swiss neutrality recognised, affirming a long-term diplomatic principle (✅ owner 05 §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Recognised neutrality (✅ owner 05 §3).
- Long-term principle (✅ owner 05 §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1176.
**Trap:** Neutrality is a recognised principle, not an alliance.
**Mains hook:** A rare settlement point that lasted.
**Back:** *"Switzerland stays neutral."*

### Flashcard WH-1184 — Holy Alliance (1815): symbolic and conservative
**Front:** What was the Holy Alliance and what was its practical importance?
**Definition (learn):** Formed in 1815 by Tsar Alexander I with Russia, Austria and Prussia; a symbolic conservative moral basis for monarchies; Britannica treats its practical importance as limited (✅ owner 05 §1, §5; ✅ Wikipedia gives 1815 and Alexander I).
**Breakdown:**
- Members: Russia, Austria, Prussia (✅ owner 05 §5).
- Moral basis: monarchism and opposition to revolution and republicanism (✅ Wikipedia).
- Symbolic and conservative (✅ owner 05 §5).
- Not the same as the Concert (WH-1186).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1815 (✅ Wikipedia); no signing date or member list in fetched text (⚠️ owner's members retained).
**Links:** WH-1185, WH-1186, WH-1187.
**Trap:** ❌ Holy Alliance = whole Concert → ✅ symbolic instrument vs broader consultation.
**Mains hook:** Use the Holy Alliance as the symbolic side of conservatism.
**Back:** *"Holy Alliance: a sermon of monarchs, not the Concert itself."*

### Flashcard WH-1185 — Concert of Europe as a practice, not a treaty-state
**Front:** What was the Concert of Europe?
**Definition (learn):** A post-Napoleonic great-power consensus favouring the status quo, worked through consultation and periodic congresses (✅ Britannica via owner 05 §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Consultation among the major powers and periodic congresses (✅ owner 05 §4).
- Intervened against revolutionary movements in Italy and Spain (✅ owner 05 §4).
- Accepted Belgian independence in 1830, showing adaptation (✅ owner 05 §4).
- Not a permanent treaty-state; a diplomatic habit backed by cooperation (⚠️ owner 05 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1830 (Belgium).
**Links:** WH-1186, WH-1188 to WH-1192, WH-1197.
**Trap:** "The Concert was purely peaceful liberal cooperation" is wrong; it often justified intervention (✅ owner 05 §5).
**Mains hook:** "Peace among powers, repression within states" as the Concert's dual face.
**Back:** *"Consultation among powers, intervention within states."*

### Flashcard WH-1186 — Holy Alliance vs Concert of Europe (close-option trap)
**Front:** How do the Holy Alliance and the Concert of Europe differ?
**Definition (learn):** The Holy Alliance was symbolic and conservative; the Concert was the broader great-power consultation system (✅ owner 05 §5, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Holy Alliance: 1815, three monarchies, symbolic (✅ owner 05 §5).
- Concert: consultation practice from 1815 into congresses (✅ owner 05 §4).
- Do not equate them (✅ owner 05 §8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1815.
**Links:** WH-1184, WH-1185.
**Trap:** This is the exam's most common Vienna close-option pair.
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction in the first two lines of any Concert answer.
**Back:** *"Alliance is a sermon; Concert is a meeting."*

### Flashcard WH-1187 — Quadruple Alliance vs Holy Alliance (overlap, not identity)
**Front:** Why must the Quadruple Alliance not be treated as the Holy Alliance?
**Definition (learn):** The two overlapped in time but were different diplomatic instruments (✅ owner 05 adv §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Holy Alliance: symbolic, three monarchies (✅ owner 05 §5).
- Quadruple Alliance: separate instrument; date not in the fetched text (⚠️ not verified).
- Owner adv §1 phase "Quadruple/Holy Alliance phase begins" 1815 (✅ owner 05 adv §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1815 (phase).
**Links:** WH-1184, WH-1186.
**Trap:** "Quadruple = Holy" is a close-option error (✅ owner 05 adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Separate instruments; do not merge.
**Back:** *"Two alliances, one year, different tools."*

### Flashcard WH-1188 — Congress of Aix-la-Chapelle (1818): France readmitted
**Front:** What did the 1818 Congress of Aix-la-Chapelle do?
**Definition (learn):** France was readmitted into great-power consultation (✅ owner 05 §1, §7.5).
**Breakdown:**
- Adaptation: readmission of the defeated power (✅ owner 05 §7.5).
- Shows the Concert could accept a defeated power back (⚠️ owner 05 §7.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1818 (✅ owner 05 §1).
**Links:** WH-1185, WH-1197.
**Trap:** Do not confuse Aix-la-Chapelle with Vienna.
**Mains hook:** The adaptation evidence for the Concert.
**Back:** *"Eighteen-eighteen: France returns to the table."*

### Flashcard WH-1189 — Carlsbad Decrees (1819): censorship and surveillance
**Front:** What did the Carlsbad Decrees (1819) impose, and against whom?
**Definition (learn):** Repressive decrees targeting liberal and nationalist politics in the German states through censorship and surveillance (✅ owner 05 §1, §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Censorship and surveillance (✅ owner 05 §5).
- Censorship, university control and investigation (✅ owner 05 adv §3).
- Targeted liberals and students (⚠️ owner 05 §7.6).
- Nationalism treated as a police problem (✅ owner 05 adv §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1819.
**Links:** WH-1173, WH-1200, WH-1196.
**Trap:** ❌ Carlsbad Decrees strengthened German democracy → ✅ they imposed censorship and surveillance.
**Mains hook:** Repression in the German states as proof of the Vienna order's legitimacy deficit.
**Back:** *"Nineteen Carlsbad: censors and spies in the German universities."*

### Flashcard WH-1190 — Troppau and Laibach (1820–21): doctrine of intervention
**Front:** What did the Troppau and Laibach congresses assert?
**Definition (learn):** The assertion that intervention against revolutions was legitimate, shown at Troppau and Laibach in 1820–21 (✅ owner 05 §1, §7.5).
**Breakdown:**
- Intervention against revolution asserted (✅ owner 05 §1).
- Concert intervened against revolutionary movements in Italy (✅ owner 05 §4).
- Armed intervention normalised (✅ owner 05 §7.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1820–21 (✅ owner 05 §1, §8.9; owner date control gives Laibach 1821).
**Links:** WH-1185, WH-1191.
**Trap:** Do not confuse intervention in Italy with a general war.
**Mains hook:** Intervention made the Concert a repression instrument.
**Back:** *"Twenty to twenty-one: intervene to restore."*

### Flashcard WH-1191 — Congress of Verona (1822): French intervention in Spain; last effective congress
**Front:** What did the 1822 Congress of Verona decide and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** Congress of Verona approved French intervention in Spain; Britain blocked wider intervention in Spanish America; it was the last major effective congress (✅ owner 05 §1, §5, adv §3).
**Breakdown:**
- French intervention in Spain approved (✅ owner 05 §1).
- Britain resisted continental policing beyond Europe (✅ owner 05 adv §3, Britannica).
- Verona was the last major effective congress (✅ owner 05 §5).
- Concert weakened unevenly; survived in modified consultation (✅ owner 05 adv §6 trap).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1822 (✅ owner 05).
**Links:** WH-1185, WH-1186, WH-1192, WH-1203.
**Trap:** Do not say the Concert "collapsed immediately" after Verona (✅ owner 05 adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Verona exposed the disagreement over intervention.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two Verona: France in Spain, Britain says no further."*

### Flashcard WH-1192 — Belgian independence (1830): the Concert adapts
**Front:** Why does Belgium's 1830 independence show the Concert's adaptability?
**Definition (learn):** The Concert accepted Belgian independence in 1830 — a secession it conceded (✅ owner 05 §4, §7.6A).
**Breakdown:**
- Belgium absorbed into a Dutch-dominated kingdom without consent (✅ owner 05 §7.6).
- Concert accepted secession; "Concert survives by compromise, not by rigid restoration" (✅ owner 05 §1).
- Demonstrated the settlement was revisable (✅ owner 05 §7.6A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1830 (✅ owner 05; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** WH-1180, WH-1185, WH-1194.
**Trap:** Belgium is the adaptation evidence, not an example of repression.
**Mains hook:** The adaptation side of the verdict.
**Back:** *"Thirty: the Concert bends for Belgium."*

### Flashcard WH-1193 — 1848: revolutions across Europe as the Vienna order's deepest challenge
**Front:** Why does 1848 matter for the Vienna settlement?
**Definition (learn):** The revolutions across Europe in 1848 posed the deepest challenge to the Vienna order (✅ owner 05 §1, §7.6A).
**Breakdown:**
- ✅ "Vienna order faces its deepest challenge" (✅ owner 05 §1).
- ✅ Liberal-national attempts fail, but the issue survives (✅ owner 05 §7.6A).
- ✅ The failed Frankfurt attempt at German unification (✅ owner 05 §7.6A; cross-ref Topic 06).
- ⚠️ Causal analysis is built from owner evidence (⚠️ owner 05 §7.6A).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1848 (✅ owner). No figures, months or leaders are to be named (⚠️ owner 05 §7.8).
**Links:** WH-1194, WH-1195, WH-1196, WH-1199.
**Trap:** Do not name 1848 leaders or constitutions (✅ owner 05 §7.8).
**Mains hook:** 1848 as the test of the postponement thesis.
**Back:** *"Forty-eight: the order is challenged, not yet toppled."*

### Flashcard WH-1194 — Why 1848 came: four convergent pressures
**Front:** What four pressures produced the 1848 revolutions?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 05 §7.6A "why they came" analysis (⚠️ causal analysis; ✅ evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- A settlement that refused the national question (Poland, Germany's 39-state confederation, Italians under Austria, Belgians absorbed) (✅ owner 05 §7.6).
- A settlement that refused the liberal question (Carlsbad, Troppau and Laibach, Verona) (✅ owner 05 §7.5).
- Precedent that the order could be forced: Belgian secession accepted 1830 (✅ owner 05 §7.6A).
- A new social question: urban wage-workers, trade unions and Chartism by the 1830s–40s; industrial and agrarian distress joined liberal and national demands with an urban crowd (✅ owner 05 §7.6A; ⚠️ continental analysis).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1830; 1819; 1820–21; 1822; 1830s–40s.
**Links:** WH-1193, WH-1136 (Chartism), WH-1132, WH-1133.
**Trap:** Do not treat 1848 as a single-cause event.
**Mains hook:** Combining constitutional and social demands is why 1848 frightened its own liberal leaders.
**Back:** *"Refused nation, refused liberty, proved revisable, added the crowd."*

### Flashcard WH-1195 — Why 1848 failed: four mechanisms
**Front:** What four mechanisms explain the failure of the 1848 revolutions?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 05 §7.6A mechanisms of failure (⚠️ analysis with ✅ evidence).
**Breakdown:**
- The coalition contained incompatible demands: liberals wanted constitutions, nationalists new states, workers and peasants social relief (⚠️ owner 05 §7.6A; ✅ basic/01 §8.5 rule).
- Nationalism divided revolutionaries: in multinational Europe, one nation's self-determination was another's dismemberment (Habsburg case) (⚠️ owner 05 §7.6A; ✅ basic/10 §8.6A).
- The armies never changed sides; dynastic armies stayed obedient (⚠️ owner 05 §7.6A; contrast Russia 1917 with basic/13 §9.4).
- Liberal nationalism had no instrument of force: Frankfurt failed; unification came in 1859–71 through armies and diplomacy (✅ owner 05 §7.6A; ✅ basic/06 §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1859–71 (cross-ref).
**Links:** WH-1193, WH-1194, WH-1196, WH-1201.
**Trap:** Do not name Frankfurt as a constitutional success — it failed (✅ owner 05 §7.6A).
**Mains hook:** Armies and diplomacy are the missing variable in 1848.
**Back:** *"Coalition split, empire clash, armies stayed, no force."*

### Flashcard WH-1196 — 1848 verdict: failed as revolution, succeeded as demonstration
**Front:** What is the connecting verdict between 1848 and unification?
**Definition (learn):** "1848 failed as a revolution and succeeded as a demonstration" (⚠️ owner 05 §7.6A verdict sentence).
**Breakdown:**
- Proved the Vienna order could no longer command consent (⚠️).
- Proved liberal nationalism could not command force alone (⚠️).
- Next generation achieved unification by borrowing force and diplomacy from the conservative states (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1848; 1859–71.
**Links:** WH-1195, WH-1193, Topic 06 cards.
**Trap:** Do not write that 1848 achieved unification directly.
**Mains hook:** This is the single cross-topic sentence connecting Vienna to unification.
**Back:** *"Forty-eight failed as revolution, won as lesson."*

### Flashcard WH-1197 — Peace or repression: a dual verdict
**Front:** Was the Vienna settlement peace or repression?
**Definition (learn):** Both, inseparably: the great-power discipline that prevented war among states was used to prevent change within them (⚠️ owner 05 §7.7).
**Breakdown:**
- Peace: no continent-wide war between 1815 and 1914 comparable to the Napoleonic Wars (⚠️ owner 05 §7.5).
- Repression: Carlsbad, Troppau and Laibach, Verona, Poland subordinated (✅ owner 05 §7.5).
- Concert: peace among powers, repression within states (✅ owner 05 adv §2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1815–1914 (⚠️ owner 05 §7.5 note); 1819; 1820–21; 1822.
**Links:** WH-1185, WH-1189, WH-1190, WH-1191.
**Trap:** Concert did not prevent all conflict; it prevented general war (⚠️ owner 05 §7.8).
**Mains hook:** Open with the dual verdict; support with two named evidence sets.
**Back:** *"Peace between states, control within them."*

### Flashcard WH-1198 — Adaptation versus rigidity: the quietly flexible order
**Front:** Why did the Vienna order last if it was not rigid?
**Definition (learn):** The order lasted because it was quietly flexible: it restored dynasties where it could and accepted change where it had to (⚠️ owner 05 §7.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Adaptation evidence: Aix-la-Chapelle 1818; Belgian independence 1830 (✅ owner 05 §7.5).
- Congress system lapsed rather than hardened (✅ owner 05 §7.5).
- Repression evidence: Carlsbad, intervention doctrine (✅ owner 05 §7.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1818; 1830.
**Links:** WH-1188, WH-1192, WH-1191.
**Trap:** Do not say the order was rigid; the evidence is adaptive as well as repressive.
**Mains hook:** The adaptation thesis rebuts the "reactionary" stereotype.
**Back:** *"Flexible where forced; rigid where it could be."*

### Flashcard WH-1199 — Deferral thesis: how far did Vienna postpone nationalism?
**Front:** How far did Vienna postpone the nationalism question?
**Definition (learn):** Vienna postponed it by roughly a generation: 1830 forced a concession, 1848 shook the order, 1859–71 completed what Vienna had denied (⚠️ owner 05 §7.2, §7.7).
**Breakdown:**
- Deferral vs suppression (⚠️ owner 05 §7.1).
- The 1830/1848 test (⚠️ owner 05 §7.1).
- Unifications 1859–71 (✅ owner 05 §7.2 cross-ref; Topic 06).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1830; 1848; 1859–71.
**Links:** WH-1193, WH-1196, WH-1202.
**Trap:** "It ignored nationalism" without the sequel is incomplete (⚠️ owner 05 §7.1).
**Mains hook:** Deferral, not suppression.
**Back:** *"Postponed a generation, not abolished."*

### Flashcard WH-1200 — Who was left out at Vienna
**Front:** Which groups were excluded from Vienna's settlement?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 05 §7.6 exclusions (✅ owner 05 §7.6; ⚠️ analytical).
**Breakdown:**
- Poles: Congress Poland under Russia (✅).
- Germans: 39-state Confederation instead of unity (✅).
- Italians: under Austrian rule in Lombardy and Venetia (✅).
- Belgians: absorbed into a Dutch-dominated kingdom without consent, reversed 1830 (✅).
- Liberals and students: targeted by the Carlsbad Decrees (⚠️).
- Peasants, workers and colonial subjects not represented at all (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1182, WH-1179, WH-1181, WH-1180, WH-1189.
**Trap:** Vienna was a settlement among dynasties and cabinets, not among peoples (⚠️ owner 05 §7.6).
**Mains hook:** Use the exclusions to convert description into evaluation.
**Back:** *"Poles, Germans, Italians, Belgians, liberals, and the people left out."*

### Flashcard WH-1201 — Repression versus adaptation ledger
**Front:** Which repressive acts and which adaptive acts define the Vienna order?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 05 §7.5 ledger (✅ evidence in each cell).
**Breakdown:**
- Repression: German liberal-national suppression (Carlsbad 1819); intervention doctrine (Troppau, Laibach 1820–21); armed intervention endorsed (Verona 1822); national aspirations subordinated (Poland, German unity postponed, Austrian influence in Italy).
- Adaptation: readmission of France (Aix 1818); acceptance of Belgian secession (1830); congress system lapsed rather than hardened (Verona last effective congress); great-power framework survived without general war for decades.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** WH-1189, WH-1190, WH-1191, WH-1188, WH-1192, WH-1197.
**Trap:** Do not use repression evidence alone to judge the whole order.
**Mains hook:** Two columns, one verdict.
**Back:** *"Carlsbad and Verona on one side, Aix and Belgium on the other."*

### Flashcard WH-1202 — Peace among states is not legitimacy among peoples
**Front:** What is the advanced formula for Vienna's achievement and deficit?
**Definition (learn):** Peace among great powers is not the same as legitimacy among peoples; Vienna succeeded at the first but struggled with the second (✅ owner 05 adv §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Shared fear of French expansion held the system (✅ owner 05 adv §3).
- Great-power consultation replaced immediate war among victors (✅ owner 05 adv §3).
- Austrian leadership in Central Europe gave conservatism a centre (✅ owner 05 adv §3).
- Repression tools: censorship, university control and investigation (✅ owner 05 adv §3).
- British limits: Britain blocked wider intervention in Spanish America at Verona; the Concert was never ideologically uniform (✅ owner 05 adv §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1197, WH-1203, WH-1199.
**Trap:** Do not say the Concert was ideologically uniform.
**Mains hook:** "Created peace among states but not legitimacy among peoples" (owner adv §7 question).
**Back:** *"Peace among states, legitimacy among peoples — Vienna had the first only."*

### Flashcard WH-1203 — The structural weakness: dynastic settlement versus social and national forces
**Front:** Why did the system weaken structurally?
**Definition (learn):** A dynastic settlement tried to contain the social and national forces released by the French Revolution; repression could not permanently reverse mass politics (⚠️ owner 05 adv §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Britannica: 1830, 1848, and Italian and German unifications made the original Concert form obsolete (✅ owner 05 adv §4).
- Verona exposed disagreement over intervention (✅ owner 05 adv §4).
- Vienna could restore rulers but not reverse public opinion and national idea (⚠️ owner 05 adv §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1830; 1848; 1822.
**Links:** WH-1191, WH-1202, WH-1193.
**Trap:** Do not say the Concert collapsed immediately (⚠️ owner 05 adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Use the structural argument in the conclusion.
**Back:** *"Rulers restored, peoples not reversed."*

### Flashcard WH-1204 — Factual-risk controls for Vienna and the Concert
**Front:** Which claims about Vienna must be avoided?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 05 §7.8 controls (✅ owner 05 §7.8).
**Breakdown:**
- Holy Alliance is not the Concert.
- Vienna did not create nation-states by popular will.
- Do not claim the settlement drew all borders permanently: Belgium (1830), Italy (1859–70), Germany (1864–71) changed them.
- German Confederation: 39 states only.
- No verbatim Metternich quotation.
- No population or area figures for transfers.
- Concert did not prevent all conflict.
- 1848: no leaders, assemblies except Frankfurt, constitutions, months, casualty or electoral figures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 39; 1830; 1859–70; 1864–71.
**Links:** WH-1184, WH-1186, WH-1179, WH-1193.
**Trap:** Check each claim against these controls.
**Mains hook:** Precision of bounds defends the answer.
**Back:** *"Thirty-nine, not permanent; no Metternich quote; no 1848 names."*

### Flashcard WH-1205 — Mark-scaled structures for Vienna
**Front:** How should 10, 15 and 20-mark Vienna answers be built?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 05 §7.3 structures (⚠️ analytic).
**Breakdown:**
- 10 marks: thesis → three principles with one territorial illustration each → one limitation → verdict.
- 15 marks: thesis → principles → territorial settlement → Concert as practice → repression record → adaptation record → graded verdict.
- 20 marks: all of the above plus long consequence chain to 1848 and unifications, and comparison with a later settlement → verdict.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1174, WH-1197, WH-1198, WH-1199.
**Trap:** The 20-mark comparison with 1919 must not assume 1919 was simply better (⚠️ owner 05 §7.1).
**Mains hook:** Use the structure to avoid a map list.
**Back:** *"Ten, fifteen, twenty — principle, practice, verdict."*

### Flashcard WH-1206 — Vienna verdict scaffolds
**Front:** What are the owner's verdict scaffolds for Vienna?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 05 §7.7 ready verdicts (⚠️ analytic).
**Breakdown:**
- Peace or repression: both, inseparably.
- How far did it postpone nationalism: roughly a generation.
- Was it a success: by its own criteria substantially; by the next century's criteria it mistook the absence of war for stability.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1197, WH-1199, WH-1203.
**Trap:** Avoid choosing one side without evidence (⚠️ owner 05 §7.1).
**Mains hook:** Graded verdict: success by its own test, failure by the century's.
**Back:** *"Both, roughly a generation, a success that mistook quiet for peace."*

### Flashcard WH-1207 — Vienna and the 2019-style comparison: 1919 vs 1815
**Front:** How should Vienna be compared with the post-1919 settlement?
**Definition (learn):** Great-power management vs self-determination (⚠️ owner 05 §7.1).
**Breakdown:**
- Vienna: legitimacy, balance, compensation, dynastic restoration (✅ owner 05 §7.4).
- Post-1919 (owned by a later topic): self-determination as principle (⚠️ owner 05 §7.1 directive).
- Do not assume 1919 was simply better (⚠️ owner 05 §7.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1815; 1919 (cross-ref to World War owner; no figures).
**Links:** WH-1174, WH-1205, WH-1206.
**Trap:** Avoid the assumption that self-determination produced better peace.
**Mains hook:** The comparative directive is a both-sides analysis.
**Back:** *"Vienna balanced kings; 1919 promised peoples — neither was simple."*

### Flashcard WH-1208 — PYQ control for Vienna and the Concert
**Front:** What is the PYQ status of the Vienna owner?
**Definition (learn):** Zero direct topic-only routes in 2018–2025; probable questions are original practice (✅ owner 05 §9).
**Breakdown:**
- No PYQ, official key or live institutional claim invented (✅ owner 05 §9).
- Probable questions: "Explain the principles..."; "Peace or repression?"; "How did Vienna postpone nationalism?" (✅ owner 05 §6).
- Boundary: Topic 03 owns Napoleon through Waterloo; Topic 06 owns unification (✅ owner 05 §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018–2025 (no direct routes).
**Links:** WH-1171, WH-1196, WH-1197.
**Trap:** Don't invent a PYQ for Vienna.
**Mains hook:** Syllabus clause: "redrawal of national boundaries" (✅ owner 05 §6).
**Back:** *"No PYQ here: practise the syllabus clause."*

### GROUP — Unification of Italy and Germany (owner 06)

### Flashcard WH-1209 — Unification as the nationalist undoing of Vienna: from above or from below
**Front:** How did Italian and German unification undo the Vienna settlement, and what is the central debate?
**Definition (learn):** Both unifications overturned the 1815 arrangement; the debate is whether national energy came from below (movements) or was executed from above (states) (✅ owner 06 §1–2; ⚠️ owner 06 adv §1 synthesis).
**Breakdown:**
- Italy: Mazzini, secret societies and Garibaldi mobilised nationalism; Piedmont and Cavour supplied the state frame (⚠️ owner 06 adv §1).
- Germany: 1848 liberals tried to make a nation; Prussia and Bismarck unified it by war and diplomacy (⚠️ owner 06 adv §1).
- Best synthesis: emotional-national energy from below; completion from above (⚠️ owner 06 adv §1).
- ✅ 1848 is the turning point: idealist nationalism survived; power passed to monarchies and ministers (⚠️ owner 06 adv §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1815 settlement; 1848 turning point (✅ owner 06 §1).
**Links:** WH-1210 (ladder), WH-1231 (evidence bank), WH-1232 (verdict), WH-1194 to WH-1196 (1848 analysis, Vienna Part).
**Trap:** Do not treat Italian and German unification as identical processes (✅ owner 06 §6).
**Mains hook:** "German unification was a revolution from above; Italian unification was a compromise between above and below." Discuss.
**Back:** *"Feeling from below, force from above, 1848 the hinge."*

### Flashcard WH-1210 — Unification chronology ladder (1815–1871)
**Front:** What are the dated stages of Italian and German unification?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's mini-timeline, one function per date (✅ owner 06 §1).
**Breakdown:**
- 1815 — Vienna leaves Italy divided and Germany in a 39-state Confederation (✅).
- 1831 — Mazzini launches Young Italy (✅ owner; Wikipedia: founded in Marseille after his 1831 release).
- 1848 — Revolutions in Europe; liberal-national attempts fail (✅).
- 1859 — Piedmont-Sardinia and France defeat Austria; Lombardy gained (✅; Wikipedia: war began April 1859).
- 1860 — Garibaldi's Red Shirts conquer Sicily and Naples (✅).
- 1861 — Kingdom of Italy proclaimed under Victor Emmanuel II (✅; Wikipedia: 17 March 1861).
- 1864 — Denmark defeated by Prussia and Austria (✅).
- 1866 — Prussia defeats Austria in Seven Weeks' War; Venetia to Italy (✅).
- 1870 — Rome joins Italy after French withdrawal (✅; Wikipedia: 1870, capital 1871).
- January 1871 — German Empire proclaimed at Versailles (✅).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** WH-1211 to WH-1228.
**Trap:** Keep the 1861 Kingdom and the 1870 Rome dates separate (✅ owner 06 §8.8).
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to show the staggered Italian and concentrated German completions.
**Back:** *"Thirty-one Mazzini, forty-eight failure, fifty-nine war, sixty-one Italy, seventy Rome, seventy-one Germany."*

### Flashcard WH-1211 — Giuseppe Mazzini and Young Italy (republican unitary nationalism)
**Front:** What did Mazzini contribute to Italian unification, and what did he not do?
**Definition (learn):** Founder of Young Italy, arguing for a united republican Italy; inspired unification without completing it (✅ owner 06 §3, §8.8).
**Breakdown:**
- Founded Young Italy (1831) — ✅ owner 06 §4 "launches Young Italy"; ✅ Wikipedia: founded in Marseille after his 1831 release.
- Republican and unitary programme (✅ owner 06 §8.4).
- Ideological inspiration (✅ owner 06 §3).
- Republican current defeated within unification; the mass movement's programme was not what was built (⚠️ owner 06 adv §8.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1831.
**Links:** WH-1212, WH-1213, WH-1214, WH-1220.
**Trap:** ❌ Mazzini completed Italian unification → ✅ Cavour, Garibaldi and Victor Emmanuel II did (✅ owner 06 §8.8).
**Mains hook:** Republican ideas defined the nation but a monarchy completed it.
**Back:** *"Mazzini dreams the republic; the monarchy builds the nation."*

### Flashcard WH-1212 — Count Cavour: diplomacy and war against Austria
**Front:** What was Cavour's role in Italian unification?
**Definition (learn):** Prime Minister of Piedmont-Sardinia who used diplomacy, reform and war with French help against Austria (✅ owner 06 §3; ✅ owner 06 adv §2).
**Breakdown:**
- Prime Minister of Piedmont-Sardinia (✅ owner 06 §3).
- Alliance with France secured the 1859 war (✅ owner 06 §8.5; ✅ Wikipedia: war began April 1859 when Cavour found an ally in Napoleon III).
- Political strategist of unification (✅ owner 06 §3).
- Italy needed an external great-power ally; Prussia did not (⚠️ owner 06 §8.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** April 1859 (Second Italian War of Independence).
**Links:** WH-1215, WH-1217, WH-1214.
**Trap:** Do not present Cavour as Garibaldi — Cavour was diplomatic, Garibaldi popular-military.
**Mains hook:** Cavour represents "from above" with a French partner.
**Back:** *"Cavour: diplomacy with Paris, war on Vienna."*

### Flashcard WH-1213 — Garibaldi and the Red Shirts (1860)
**Front:** What did Garibaldi's Red Shirts achieve?
**Definition (learn):** The Red Shirts conquered Sicily and Naples in 1860, bringing southern Italy into unification (✅ owner 06 §3, §8.4).
**Breakdown:**
- Popular-military energy from below (✅ owner 06 §3).
- Volunteer-revolutionary hero; not a state strategist (✅ owner 06 adv §6 trap).
- Single sharpest contrast with Germany: no equivalent mass military hero (✅ owner 06 §8.4).
- Monarchy absorbed the popular gain (⚠️ owner 06 §8.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1860; Sicily and Naples (✅ owner 06 §3).
**Links:** WH-1216, WH-1214, WH-1223.
**Trap:** Do not put Garibaldi and Bismarck in the same role (✅ owner 06 adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Garibaldi's campaign is the mass element that Germany lacked.
**Back:** *"Red Shirts take the south in sixty."*

### Flashcard WH-1214 — Victor Emmanuel II and the Kingdom of Italy (1861)
**Front:** What role did Victor Emmanuel II play and when was the Kingdom proclaimed?
**Definition (learn):** King of Piedmont-Sardinia and then king of united Italy; the Kingdom of Italy proclaimed in 1861 (✅ owner 06 §3, §6; ✅ Wikipedia: 17 March 1861).
**Breakdown:**
- Monarchical centre of unification (✅ owner 06 §3).
- Monarchy completed the process; republican currents were displaced (⚠️ owner 06 adv §6).
- Kingdom of Italy: 1861; Rome not until 1870 (✅ owner 06 §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1861; 17 March (Wikipedia).
**Links:** WH-1211, WH-1212, WH-1218.
**Trap:** ❌ "Rome joined Italy in 1861" → ✅ Rome joined in 1870 (✅ owner 06 §8.8).
**Mains hook:** The monarchy completed what the republic began.
**Back:** *"Sixty-one Victor Emmanuel, seventy Rome."*

### Flashcard WH-1215 — Second Italian War of Independence (1859): Piedmont and France against Austria
**Front:** What did the 1859 war achieve for Italian unification?
**Definition (learn):** Piedmont-Sardinia and France defeated Austria; Lombardy gained; unification moved from ideas to state action (✅ owner 06 §1; ✅ Wikipedia: began April 1859).
**Breakdown:**
- Piedmont-Sardinia and France defeat Austria (✅ owner 06 §1).
- Lombardy gained (✅ owner 06 §1).
- French help mattered in 1859 (✅ owner 06 adv §4).
- Austria's decisive defeat (✅ owner 06 §8.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** April 1859 (✅ Wikipedia); 1859 (✅ owner).
**Links:** WH-1212, WH-1217.
**Trap:** Venetia came later (1866), not in 1859 (✅ owner 06 §8.8).
**Mains hook:** The 1859 war is the moment ideas became state action.
**Back:** *"Fifty-nine: Lombardy freed with French arms."*

### Flashcard WH-1216 — Southern conquest (1860): Naples and Sicily
**Front:** Why was the 1860 conquest of Sicily and Naples decisive for Italy?
**Definition (learn):** The popular-military campaign that brought southern Italy into the unification process (✅ owner 06 §1, §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Sicily and Naples conquered by the Red Shirts (✅ owner 06 §3).
- Southern Italy joins the process (✅ owner 06 §1).
- Unification joined a commercialising north to a far poorer south — the "southern question" (⚠️ owner 06 adv §8.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1860 (✅ owner 06).
**Links:** WH-1213, WH-1221.
**Trap:** Do not cite income or literacy figures for the north–south gap (⚠️ owner 06 §8.6).
**Mains hook:** Popular conquest and the southern question are linked.
**Back:** *"Sixty: the south comes in, the question stays."*

### Flashcard WH-1217 — Venetia (1866): the Third War of Independence
**Front:** How did Venetia join Italy?
**Definition (learn):** Venetia joined Italy in 1866, after Prussia defeated Austria in the Seven Weeks' War (✅ owner 06 §1, §4; ✅ Wikipedia TOC: "Third War of Independence (1866)").
**Breakdown:**
- Venetia to Italy 1866 (✅ owner 06 §1).
- Italy advances because Austria is excluded (✅ owner 06 §1).
- Venetia gained through Austria's defeat (✅ owner 06 §8.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1866.
**Links:** WH-1225, WH-1218, WH-1215.
**Trap:** Do not confuse Venetia (1866) with Lombardy (1859).
**Mains hook:** A German war gave Italy its last northern province.
**Back:** *"Sixty-six: Venetia rides on Prussia's victory."*

### Flashcard WH-1218 — Rome (1870) and the Papal question
**Front:** When and how did Rome join Italy, and what persisted afterwards?
**Definition (learn):** Rome joined Italy in 1870 after French withdrawal; Rome became capital in 1871 (✅ owner 06 §1, §6; ✅ Wikipedia: 1870; capital 1871).
**Breakdown:**
- Rome joined in 1870 after French withdrawal (✅ owner 06 §1).
- Papacy's relationship with the new state remained hostile for decades (⚠️ owner 06 §8.6).
- Formal settlement: Lateran Treaty 1929 (✅ owner 06 §8.6 cross-ref to basic/12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1870; 1871 capital; 1929 Lateran (cross-ref).
**Links:** WH-1219, WH-1221, WH-1214.
**Trap:** Do not date Rome to 1861 (✅ owner 06 §8.8).
**Mains hook:** The Roman question shows integration remained incomplete.
**Back:** *"Seventy: French leave, Rome comes in."*

### Flashcard WH-1219 — Piedmont-Sardinia as the nucleus of Italian unification
**Front:** Why was Piedmont-Sardinia the nucleus?
**Definition (learn):** NCERT and Britannica emphasise Piedmont-Sardinia as the nucleus of unification (✅ owner 06 §3).
**Breakdown:**
- Strengthened at Vienna by Genoa (✅ owner 05 §3; owner 06 §8.4).
- Built a state frame with reform and diplomacy (✅ owner 06 adv §2).
- The Vienna gift created the core that undid Vienna (⚠️ owner 06 §8.4; WH-1178).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Genoa (Vienna 1815).
**Links:** WH-1178, WH-1212, WH-1181.
**Trap:** Do not present Piedmont as a republic.
**Mains hook:** Vienna created the nucleus that undid it.
**Back:** *"Vienna's gift became Italy's nucleus."*

### Flashcard WH-1220 — Italy's republican defeat and the monarchy's victory
**Front:** Why does the Italian case show that republican nationalism was defeated within unification?
**Definition (learn):** Mazzini's republic was displaced by a monarchy; the mass movement's own programme was not what was built (⚠️ owner 06 adv §8.6; ✅ owner 06 adv §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Republican currents mattered; monarchy completed the process (✅ owner 06 adv §6).
- The monarchy absorbed the popular conquests (⚠️ owner 06 §8.5).
- Italy visibly combined Mazzini's programme, Cavour's statecraft, Garibaldi's arms and Victor Emmanuel II's monarchy (✅ owner 06 §9 mechanism control).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1861.
**Links:** WH-1211, WH-1214, WH-1240.
**Trap:** ❌ Italian unification was purely republican → ✅ monarchy completed it.
**Mains hook:** Use the four-element list to explain Italy's combination.
**Back:** *"Mazzini's republic yielded to the king."*

### Flashcard WH-1221 — Italy's north–south divide and the southern question
**Front:** What limits did Italian unification leave?
**Definition (learn):** Unification joined a commercialising north to a far poorer south under northern institutions; the "southern question" became a permanent feature (⚠️ owner 06 §8.6).
**Breakdown:**
- Keep qualitative; no income or literacy figures (⚠️ owner 06 §8.6).
- Regional divides and the Roman question persisted (✅ owner 06 adv §2).
- Political unification did not mean social integration (⚠️ owner 06 adv §2).
- "Mezzogiorno" only as an analytical reference (⚠️ owner 06 §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1216, WH-1218, WH-1236.
**Trap:** Do not present Italy as fully homogenised (⚠️ owner 06 adv §2).
**Mains hook:** A mature answer says unified but not homogenised.
**Back:** *"Unified, not homogenised: north, south, pope."*

### Flashcard WH-1222 — German Confederation and the 1848 Frankfurt failure
**Front:** What was the German Confederation and why did the Frankfurt attempt fail?
**Definition (learn):** A confederation of 39 states under Austrian dominance; the Frankfurt liberal attempt at German unification failed in 1848 (✅ owner 06 §2, §8.4; ✅ owner 05 §7.6A).
**Breakdown:**
- 39 states under Austrian dominance after Vienna (✅ owner 06 adv §3).
- Frankfurt attempt failed; pushed unification toward statecraft, diplomacy and war (✅ owner 06 §2).
- Germany's liberal model lacked an instrument of force (✅ owner 05 §7.6A mechanism 4).
- German unity after 1871 was "small German" under Prussia (✅ owner 06 adv §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 39 states; 1848 (✅).
**Links:** WH-1179, WH-1193, WH-1195, WH-1224.
**Trap:** Do not claim Frankfurt achieved unification (✅ owner 05 §7.6A).
**Mains hook:** The Frankfurt failure is the German turning point.
**Back:** *"Thirty-nine states, Frankfurt fails, Prussia takes the lead."*

### Flashcard WH-1223 — Otto von Bismarck: minister-president (1862) and Realpolitik
**Front:** Who was Bismarck and what method did he use?
**Definition (learn):** Prussia's minister-president from 1862 who pursued practical power politics; the central architect of state-led German unification (✅ owner 06 §4; ✅ Britannica via owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Minister-president of Prussia from 1862 (✅ owner 06 §4, §8.8).
- Realpolitik: politics guided by practical state interest rather than abstract principle (⚠️ owner 06 §4 definition).
- Used nationalism instrumentally under Prussian leadership (✅ owner 06 adv §6).
- Bismarck's three wars (1864, 1866, 1870–71) (✅ owner 06 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1862; January 1871 (Empire).
**Links:** WH-1224, WH-1225, WH-1226, WH-1228.
**Trap:** ❌ Bismarck created nationalism → ✅ he used it (✅ owner 06 adv §6).
**Mains hook:** Bismarck as the state strategist, not the mass hero.
**Back:** *"Sixty-two Bismarck: power over principle."*

### Flashcard WH-1224 — Danish War (1864): the Schleswig-Holstein opening
**Front:** What did the 1864 Danish War open?
**Definition (learn):** Denmark defeated by Prussia and Austria; the Schleswig-Holstein question opened the path to unification (✅ owner 06 §1, §4).
**Breakdown:**
- Defeat of Denmark by Prussia and Austria (✅ owner 06 §1).
- First of Bismarck's three wars (✅ owner 06 §1).
- Schleswig-Holstein question (✅ owner 06 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1864.
**Links:** WH-1223, WH-1225, WH-1229.
**Trap:** Denmark was not an ally of Austria; the owner lists the defeat by both.
**Mains hook:** The first war in the sequence.
**Back:** *"Sixty-four: Denmark first, Austria next."*

### Flashcard WH-1225 — Seven Weeks' War (1866): Austria excluded
**Front:** What did the Seven Weeks' War of 1866 achieve?
**Definition (learn):** Prussia defeated Austria; Austria excluded from German affairs and the North German Confederation emerged (✅ owner 06 §4, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Austria excluded (✅ owner 06 §4).
- North German Confederation emerged (✅ owner 06 §4).
- "Little Germany" under Prussia prevailed (✅ owner 06 adv §3).
- Venetia to Italy in 1866 (✅ owner 06 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1866.
**Links:** WH-1217, WH-1223, WH-1237.
**Trap:** Seven Weeks' War ≠ Franco-Prussian War (1870–71).
**Mains hook:** Austria's exclusion is the common precondition of both unifications (✅ owner 06 §8.4).
**Back:** *"Sixty-six: Austria out, Little Germany in."*

### Flashcard WH-1226 — Franco-Prussian (Franco-German) War (1870–71)
**Front:** What did the Franco-German War achieve for Germany, and what did France lose?
**Definition (learn):** South German states rallied to Prussia; the empire was proclaimed after the war; Alsace-Lorraine lost by France in 1871 (✅ owner 06 §4, §8.6).
**Breakdown:**
- South German states rally to Prussia (✅ owner 06 §4, §8.5).
- War manufactured the national moment (⚠️ owner 06 §8.5).
- France: Alsace-Lorraine lost in 1871 (✅ owner 06 §8.6); French revanchism a named source of 1914 friction (✅ owner 06 §8.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1870–71; 1871 (Alsace-Lorraine).
**Links:** WH-1227, WH-1238, WH-1229.
**Trap:** Do not place Alsace-Lorraine under Prussia before 1871 (⚠️ owner 06 §8.6).
**Mains hook:** The war that made the empire created the French grievance.
**Back:** *"Seventy: the south joins, Alsace goes."*

### Flashcard WH-1227 — German Empire proclaimed at Versailles (January 1871)
**Front:** When and where was the German Empire proclaimed and who became emperor?
**Definition (learn):** The German Empire was proclaimed at Versailles in January 1871; William I of Prussia became emperor (✅ owner 06 §4, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Date: January 1871 (✅ owner 06 §1).
- Place: Versailles (✅ owner 06 §4).
- William I of Prussia as emperor (✅ owner 06 §4).
- National unity with Prussian military prestige (✅ owner 06 adv §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** January 1871.
**Links:** WH-1226, WH-1223.
**Trap:** Do not say Germany was unified by popular assemblies; Prussian power and war were decisive (✅ owner 06 §8.8).
**Mains hook:** The proclamation is the formal end, not the social integration.
**Back:** *"January seventy-one: Versailles, William, empire."*

### Flashcard WH-1228 — Realpolitik defined
**Front:** What is Realpolitik and how does it explain Bismarck?
**Definition (learn):** Politics guided by practical state interest rather than abstract principle (⚠️ owner 06 §4, §8.5).
**Breakdown:**
- Explains why Bismarck could ally with liberal nationalism without sharing its aims (⚠️ owner 06 §8.5).
- Do not quote Bismarck verbatim ("blood and iron" or otherwise); paraphrase (✅ owner 06 §8.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1223, WH-1230.
**Trap:** Do not put Realpolitik in Bismarck's mouth as a quotation.
**Mains hook:** Use Realpolitik to explain method, not morality.
**Back:** *"Practical interest over abstract principle — paraphrase only."*

### Flashcard WH-1229 — Germany's three wars in sequence
**Front:** What were Bismarck's three wars, in order?
**Definition (learn):** The Danish War (1864), the Austro-Prussian War (Seven Weeks', 1866) and the Franco-German War (1870–71) (✅ owner 06 §4, §6).
**Breakdown:**
- 1864: Denmark; Schleswig-Holstein opened (✅).
- 1866: Austria; Austria excluded; North German Confederation (✅).
- 1870–71: France; South German states rally; empire proclaimed (✅).
- German unification ladder: Prussian power → Bismarck's wars → Austria excluded → Empire proclaimed 1871 (✅ owner 06 §4 diagram).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1864; 1866; 1870–71.
**Links:** WH-1224, WH-1225, WH-1226.
**Trap:** Do not list three wars against Austria; the third was against France.
**Mains hook:** War became the instrument of nation-building.
**Back:** *"Denmark, Austria, France — sixty-four, sixty-six, seventy."*

### Flashcard WH-1230 — Italy vs Germany: five-axis comparison
**Front:** How do Italian and German unification compare on core state, strategist, mass element, founder and completion?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 06 §5 and §8.4 comparison (✅ facts; ⚠️ analytical reading).
**Breakdown:**
- Core state: Piedmont-Sardinia (Italy) vs Prussia (Germany).
- Strategist: Cavour vs Bismarck.
- Mass element: Garibaldi vs no equivalent mass military hero.
- Ideological founder: Mazzini's Young Italy vs no equivalent (1848 Frankfurt failed).
- Completion: Italy staggered (1861, 1866, 1870); Germany concentrated in seven years (1864–71).
- Character: Italy mixed; Germany heavily state-driven and military.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dates as above.
**Links:** WH-1211 to WH-1218, WH-1222 to WH-1227.
**Trap:** Do not treat them as two parallel narratives (✅ owner 06 §8.1).
**Mains hook:** The 10-mark comparison uses two axes: core state and method.
**Back:** *"Same enemy, different core, different method."*

### Flashcard WH-1231 — From below versus from above: evidence bank
**Front:** Which evidence supports "from below" and which supports "from above" for each unification?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 06 §8.5 bank (✅ evidence; ⚠️ analytic labels).
**Breakdown:**
- From below: Young Italy (1831); Red Shirts conquer south (1860); 1848 revolutions put unity on agenda.
- From above: Cavour's alliance with France (1859); Kingdom under Victor Emmanuel II (1861); after 1848 unification moved to diplomacy and war; Bismarck's three wars (1864, 1866, 1870–71).
- War manufactured the national moment in Germany (⚠️ owner 06 §8.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1831; 1848; 1859; 1860; 1861; 1864; 1866; 1870–71.
**Links:** WH-1209, WH-1232, WH-1211, WH-1212.
**Trap:** Do not give one verdict for both countries (✅ owner 06 §8.1).
**Mains hook:** Build a two-column evidence table per country.
**Back:** *"Below ignites, above executes."*

### Flashcard WH-1232 — Ideas or state power? Verdict
**Front:** What is the verdict on whether unification was achieved by ideas or by state power?
**Definition (learn):** Ideas made unification thinkable and legitimate; state power made it happen; the gap explains why unified states disappointed nationalists (⚠️ owner 06 §8.7).
**Breakdown:**
- Ideas: legitimacy (WH-1211 Mazzini; WH-1222 1848).
- State power: diplomacy, war, monarchy (WH-1212, WH-1223).
- Gap: disappointed nationalists (⚠️ owner 06 §8.7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None.
**Links:** WH-1209, WH-1231, WH-1196.
**Trap:** Do not choose one side without evidence (⚠️ owner 06 §8.1).
**Mains hook:** Use as the conclusion for the "ideas or state power?" question.
**Back:** *"Ideas made it thinkable; state power made it real."*

### Flashcard WH-1233 — Staggered Italian completion and concentrated German completion
**Front:** How did the completion of Italy differ from Germany's?
**Definition (learn):** Italy's completion staggered over 1861, 1866 and 1870; Germany's concentrated in seven years (⚠️ owner 06 §8.4 reading).
**Breakdown:**
- Italy: Kingdom 1861; Venetia 1866; Rome 1870 (✅ owner 06 §8.4).
- Germany: 1864 to January 1871 (✅ owner 06 §8.4).
- Consequence: Italy a middling power with weak state capacity; Germany the strongest continental military-economic power (⚠️ owner 06 §8.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1861; 1866; 1870; 1871.
**Links:** WH-1230, WH-1234.
**Trap:** Do not make Italy's power the same as Germany's.
**Mains hook:** Power consequences feed the 1914 problem.
**Back:** *"Italy staggered; Germany concentrated and too strong."*

### Flashcard WH-1234 — European balance consequences of unification (feeds 1914)
**Front:** How did the two unifications reshape the European balance?
