**Mains hook:** "Did the Gulf War confirm a new world order?" Answer with Council alignment and its limits.
**Back:** *"Twenty-nine November: all necessary means, not a blue helmet."*

### Flashcard WH-4084 — Gulf War (1990–91): coalition enforcement, not peacekeeping
**Front:** How should the Gulf War of 1990–91 be categorised?
**Definition (learn):** Iraq's invasion of Kuwait was reversed by a broad US-led coalition under Security Council authority. It was coalition enforcement, not a UN-commanded blue-helmet operation.
**Breakdown:**
- Iraq's invasion of Kuwait reversed by US-led coalition (✅ owner 21 §7).
- Legal basis: Resolution 678 (WH-4083) (✅ owner 21 §14.7).
- Limit: an interstate coalition does not solve civil wars or state collapse (✅ owner 21 §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1990–91 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** WH-4083, WH-4085, WH-4088.
**Trap:** The "blue-helmet trap" in examiner answers (✅ owner 21 §14.7). Coalition action was not a permanent UN army (❌ owner 21 adv §10).
**Mains hook:** Use as the first test of the new order: alignment achieved; general solution not.
**Back:** *"Coalition under the Council's flag — the Gulf was enforced, not policed."*

### Flashcard WH-4085 — Unipolarity versus order: capability is not legitimacy
**Front:** What is the owner's distinction between unipolarity and order?
**Definition (learn):** Unipolarity is a distribution of capability. Order additionally requires rules, legitimacy, institutional consent and the ability to manage different kinds of conflict.
**Breakdown:**
- The USSR disappeared; the USA had unmatched military reach (✅ owner 21 §7).
- Predominant capability did not create universal legitimacy or control (✅ owner 21 §7).
- Liberal-order confidence: markets, elections and institutions appeared to expand together (⚠️ owner 21 §7).
- Better phrase: "a unipolar moment inside a still plural and unequal international society" (⚠️ owner 21 adv §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** WH-4083, WH-4084, WH-4086, WH-4096.
**Trap:** "US unipolarity meant uncontested world government" is wrong (❌ owner 21 adv §10).
**Mains hook:** "Critically examine the idea of a 'new world order' after 1991." Qualify: unipolar in capability, plural in sovereignty and legitimacy (⚠️ owner 21 §14.2).
**Back:** *"Power can be unipolar; order needs consent as well as guns."*

### Flashcard WH-4086 — Yugoslavia: nationalism as political production
**Front:** What drove the Yugoslav wars, according to the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The end of communism interacted with aggressive nationalism and bad leadership. Milošević's Serbia, Tuđman's Croatia and Bosnia's mixed population made compromise difficult. Political entrepreneurs found ethnic mobilisation the cheapest route to power (⚠️ owner 21 §14.2 formulation).
**Breakdown:**
- Institutional dissolution: federal framework failed before violence (✅ owner 21 §14.6).
- Leaders: Milošević (Serbia), Tuđman (Croatia) (✅ owner 21 §8, §14.6).
- Bosnia: mixed population (✅ owner 21 §8).
- International hesitation before stronger intervention (✅ owner 21 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Wars of breakup 1991–95 (✅ owner 21 mini-timeline).
**Links:** WH-4087, WH-4088, WH-4089.
**Trap:** Do not explain Yugoslavia by "ancient hatreds"; leadership, institutions and external hesitation mattered (❌ owner 21 §11).
**Mains hook:** "Discuss the breakup of Yugoslavia as a post-Cold-War crisis of nationalism and state failure."
**Back:** *"Leaders found nationalism cheap; the federation found no defender."*

### Flashcard WH-4087 — Bosnia and the Dayton settlement (1995)
**Front:** What did the Dayton settlement of 1995 end, and what did it leave open?
**Definition (learn):** Dayton (1995) ended the Bosnian war but did not settle Kosovo. Bosnia witnessed ethnic cleansing, huge refugee flows and international hesitation before stronger intervention.
**Breakdown:**
- Dayton 1995 ended the Bosnian war (✅ owner 21 §8).
- Ethnic cleansing and refugee flows (✅ owner 21 §8, §14.6).
- Frozen conflicts are not resolved conflicts (✅ owner 21 §14.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1995 (✅ owner 21). No casualty or refugee figures in owner; none to be added (❌ owner 21 §14.11).
**Links:** WH-4086, WH-4088.
**Trap:** Do not say Dayton settled Kosovo (❌ owner 21 §8).
**Mains hook:** Use Bosnia as the clearest post-1945 European case of forced displacement (✅ owner 21 §14.6).
**Back:** *"Dayton stopped the Bosnian guns and left Kosovo's door open."*

### Flashcard WH-4088 — Kosovo and NATO's 1999 intervention
**Front:** What did NATO's 1999 Kosovo intervention show about the alliance and about authorisation?
**Definition (learn):** NATO's 1999 Kosovo intervention showed the alliance moving beyond territorial defence while exposing disputes over authorisation, sovereignty and humanitarian claims.
**Breakdown:**
- 1999 NATO intervention (✅ owner 21 §8).
- Kosovo crisis (✅ owner 21 mini-timeline).
- Dispute: authorisation, sovereignty and humanitarian justification (✅ owner 21 §8).
- Lowe: UN response hesitant, then NATO acted more decisively (✅ owner 21 adv §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1999 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** WH-4086, WH-4087, WH-4084.
**Trap:** NATO's Balkan intervention does not prove the UN system worked smoothly (❌ owner 21 adv §10).
**Mains hook:** "NATO's Kosovo intervention: alliance expansion of role or humanitarian necessity?" Qualify both sides (⚠️ owner 21 §8).
**Back:** *"Ninety-nine: NATO left the defence line and argued about its legal coat."*

### Flashcard WH-4089 — Trap: "ancient hatreds" and Yugoslavia
**Front:** Why is "ancient hatreds" the wrong explanation for the Yugoslav breakup?
**Definition (learn):** The owner does not treat Yugoslavia as an inevitable explosion of old hatred. It stresses political choices, institutions and external hesitation.
**Breakdown:**
- Leadership choices and aggressive nationalism (✅ owner 21 adv §4).
- Institutions: federal dissolution before violence (✅ owner 21 §14.6).
- External hesitation before stronger intervention (✅ owner 21 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** WH-4086, WH-4087.
**Trap:** Naming "ancient hatreds" as the cause is a defeated answer (❌ owner 21 §14.1).
**Mains hook:** Use WH-4086's formulation as the thesis.
**Back:** *"Not ancient hatred — recent politics with old maps."*

### Flashcard WH-4090 — European integration roots: ECSC (1951–52) and the Treaty of Rome (1957)
**Front:** What were the first two stages of European integration, and why does the owner insist on their roots?
**Definition (learn):** The European Coal and Steel Community (1951–52) pooled strategic industries to make renewed Franco-German war less likely. The Treaty of Rome (1957) created the European Economic Community with six states.
**Breakdown:**
- ECSC 1951–52: security policy by economic means (✅ owner 21 §9, §14.8).
- Treaty of Rome 1957: six states; common market (✅ owner 21 §9, §14.8).
- Roots: integration did not begin after the Cold War (✅ owner 21 §14.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1951–52; 1957 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** WH-4091, WH-4092, WH-4093.
**Trap:** Do not say European integration began after the Cold War (❌ owner 21 §14.11).
**Mains hook:** "Was European integration driven more by peace, economics or geopolitical strategy?" Start at the 1950s roots.
**Back:** *"Coal and steel in fifty-one, a common market by fifty-seven."*

### Flashcard WH-4091 — Maastricht Treaty: signed 7 February 1992, in force 1 November 1993
**Front:** When was the Maastricht Treaty signed and when did it enter into force?
**Definition (learn):** The Treaty on European Union (Maastricht Treaty) added a broader political and monetary framework to the European project, and it is described as the foundation treaty of the EU.
**Breakdown:**
- Signed 7 February 1992 in Maastricht, Netherlands (✅ Wikipedia, "Maastricht Treaty").
- Entered into force 1 November 1993 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner: "Maastricht (1992–93) … broader political and monetary framework" (✅ owner 21 §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 7 Feb 1992; 1 Nov 1993 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** WH-4090, WH-4092, WH-4093.
**Trap:** "EU is simply the old EEC with a new name" is wrong (❌ owner 21 adv §10). Do not confuse the signing year with the entry-into-force year.
**Mains hook:** Deepening reaches sovereignty questions directly (✅ owner 21 §14.8).
**Back:** *"Ninety-two signed in Maastricht; ninety-three in force — the Union began."*

### Flashcard WH-4092 — Enlargement: first (1973) and eastern (2004)
**Front:** What were the first enlargement of 1973 and the eastern enlargement of 2004?
**Definition (learn):** In 1973 Britain, Ireland and Denmark joined the Community. In 2004 former communist states entered the Union, linking integration to the post-Cold-War settlement.
**Breakdown:**
- 1973: Britain, Ireland, Denmark (✅ owner 21 §9).
- 2004: former communist states (✅ owner 21 §9).
- Tension: widening competes with deepening (✅ owner 21 §14.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1973; 2004 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** WH-4090, WH-4091, WH-4093.
**Trap:** Do not add the membership numbers of 2004; none sourced (❌ owner 21 §14.11).
**Mains hook:** "Explain how European integration changed from a Cold-War peace project into a post-Cold-War enlargement project."
**Back:** *"Seventy-three widened it; oh-four rebuilt the east."*

### Flashcard WH-4093 — European integration: peace project, market bargain, sovereignty question
**Front:** What is the advanced synthesis of European integration?
**Definition (learn):** Integration was neither an inevitable federal march nor a mere free-trade arrangement. It was a repeatedly renegotiated bargain among peace, prosperity, national sovereignty and geopolitical anchoring.
**Breakdown:**
- Peace-through-integration (ECSC), with NATO and US security backing also mattering (✅ owner 21 adv §6).
- Economic integration: EEC common market; uneven costs (✅ owner 21 adv §6).
- Supranational deepening: Maastricht, monetary union (✅ owner 21 adv §6).
- Widening: 2004 incorporated many former communist states; institutional and distributive tensions (✅ owner 21 adv §6).
- Member states retained major powers (✅ owner 21 adv §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above.
**Links:** WH-4090 to WH-4092.
**Trap:** Do not say the EU is an inevitable federal state; the owner rejects a smooth march (❌ owner 21 §14.8).
**Mains hook:** "Was European integration driven more by peace, economics or geopolitical strategy?" Answer: a renegotiated bargain; the weight changes by period.
**Back:** *"Peace, prosperity, sovereignty and anchoring — renegotiated every decade."*

### Flashcard WH-4094 — Global South and India after 1991: recalibration, not passivity
**Front:** How does the owner read postcolonial and Indian agency in the new order?
**Definition (learn):** The end of bipolarity did not make postcolonial states passive recipients. They bargained through regional organisations, the UN, commodity and trade coalitions, and issue-based partnerships. For India, 1991 joined the loss of the Soviet strategic setting with a balance-of-payments crisis.
**Breakdown:**
- Postcolonial sovereignty: hierarchy, debt and intervention remained (⚠️ owner 21 §14.9).
- "Global South" is an analytical category, not one bloc (✅ owner 21 adv §8).
- India: strategic and economic recalibration; diversified partnerships; decision-making autonomy (⚠️ owner 21 adv §8).
- Cross-links: Economy (1991 package) and IR basic/01 (strategic autonomy) (✅ owner 21 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1991 (✅ owner 21).
**Links:** WH-4066, WH-4033, WH-4083.
**Trap:** Do not portray the Global South as a single bloc (❌ owner 21 §14.9). Do not duplicate India's 1991 reform detail (⚠️ owner 21 adv §8).
**Mains hook:** "Assess the US unipolar moment and its limits from a Global South perspective."
**Back:** *"Bipolarity ended; the South kept bargaining — and India recalibrated in 1991."*

### Flashcard WH-4095 — Fukuyama, "The End of History?" (1989) — ⚠️ not in owner 21
**Front:** What did Francis Fukuyama argue in "The End of History?", and when?
**Definition (learn):** The spread of Western liberal democracy after the Cold War marks the endpoint of humanity's ideological development, and future events would only make democracy more widespread (✅ Wikipedia, "The End of History and the Last Man").
**Breakdown:**
- Essay: "The End of History?", The National Interest, summer 1989 (✅ Wikipedia; the title carries a question mark).
- Book: The End of History and the Last Man, 1992, Free Press (✅ Wikipedia).
- Core claim: liberal democracy as the final form of government (✅ Wikipedia).
- Not named in owner 21; cross-check against the teaching owners before using (⚠️ owner gap).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Summer 1989 essay; 1992 book (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** WH-4069, WH-4096, WH-4098 (non-teleology), WH-4085.
**Trap:** Do not present Fukuyama as an owner-verified claim; the owner's rule is non-teleology (⚠️ owner 21 §14.2). Do not attribute the 2026 or later outcomes to him.
**Mains hook:** "The collapse of communism proved liberal democracy inevitable?" Refute with the non-teleology evidence: Yugoslavia, Russia, Global South (⚠️ owner 21 §14.10).
**Back:** *"Eighty-nine asked the question; ninety-two wrote the book — and history kept going."*

### Flashcard WH-4096 — Huntington, "The Clash of Civilizations?" (1993) — ⚠️ not in owner 21
**Front:** What did Samuel P. Huntington argue in "The Clash of Civilizations?" and when?
**Definition (learn):** After the Cold War, cultural and religious identities, rather than ideology, would drive most global conflict.
**Breakdown:**
- Article: "The Clash of Civilizations?", Foreign Affairs, 1993 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Book: 1996 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Civilizations named in the book include Western, Orthodox, Islamic, Sinic, Latin American, Sub-Saharan African, Hindu, Buddhist, Japanese (✅ Wikipedia; a "possible eighth civilization" is noted; no total stated).
- Not named in owner 21; cross-check before use (⚠️ owner gap).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1993 article; 1996 book (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** WH-4095, WH-4085, WH-4094.
**Trap:** Do not state a fixed number of civilizations; the page does not give one (✅ Wikipedia). Do not treat Huntington's thesis as an owner-endorsed explanation.
**Mains hook:** Use as a counter-thesis to Fukuyama: identity rather than ideology as the post-Cold-War conflict axis (⚠️ analytical use).
**Back:** *"Ninety-three: identity, not ideology, draws the next fault lines."*

### Flashcard WH-4097 — Historiographical schools on the Cold War's end
**Front:** What five readings of the Cold War's end does the owner list, and what does each explain?
**Definition (learn):** Western-triumphalist (arms pressure and US resolve); structuralist (stagnation, technology, nationality contradictions); agency-centred (Gorbachev, Yeltsin, Reagan); social-history (workers, churches, civic networks); postcolonial (questions one Western model as endpoint).
**Breakdown:**
- Triumphalist: explains an external constraint but understates Soviet and Eastern European agency (⚠️ owner 21 adv §1).
- Structuralist: explains vulnerability but not why collapse came in 1989–91 rather than earlier (⚠️ owner 21 adv §1).
- Agency-centred: explains decisions but can exaggerate freedom from structures (⚠️ owner 21 adv §1).
- Social-history: must distinguish Poland, Hungary, East Germany, Czechoslovakia and Romania (⚠️ owner 21 adv §1).
- Postcolonial: questions a universal endpoint; attention to hierarchy and unequal globalisation (⚠️ owner 21 adv §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** WH-4073, WH-4095, WH-4096.
**Trap:** Naming one school as "the answer" is the historiography trap (⚠️ owner 21 §12).
**Mains hook:** Test each thesis against named evidence (⚠️ owner 21 §12).
**Back:** *"Five lenses: arms, structure, leaders, society, the South."*

### Flashcard WH-4098 — Doctrine trap: the collapse of communism did not prove socialism failed
**Front:** Why must a 1989–91 answer not say that "socialism failed"?
**Definition (learn):** What collapsed was a regime type. Communism, socialism and social democracy are distinct doctrines and regime forms. Doctrinal conclusions require the distinction first.
**Breakdown:**
- Regime type collapsed, not socialism as an idea (⚠️ owner 21 §8).
- Required prior reading: World History basic/01 §9.2 (socialism ≠ communism ≠ social democracy) (✅ owner 21 §14.11 cross-link).
- Cross-link: Basic/13 §9.7 on nationality and ideology (✅ owner 21 §14.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** WH-4072, WH-4095, WH-4065.
**Trap:** "The collapse of communism proved socialism failed" is a defeated doctrinal claim (❌ owner 21 §14.11).
**Mains hook:** Use the regime-type distinction to answer "what did 1989–91 disprove?"
**Back:** *"A regime fell; the idea was never on trial in 1991."*

### Flashcard WH-4099 — Non-teleology: outcomes depended on institutions, not one end-state
**Front:** What does non-teleology mean in this topic?
**Definition (learn):** The collapse of Soviet communism was not proof that every state would converge on one political-economic model. Outcomes depended on institutions, social coalitions, state capacity and external constraints (⚠️ owner 21 §14.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Liberal convergence was a claim, not a predetermined law (✅ owner 21 §14.9).
- Evidence: divergent post-Soviet and Yugoslav outcomes (⚠️ owner 21 §14.9).
- Rule for answers: no end-of-history teleology invented (✅ owner 21 adv §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** WH-4095, WH-4096, WH-4085.
**Trap:** Treating 1991 as the final endpoint is the teleology trap (❌ owner 21 adv §10).
**Mains hook:** "The Cold War ended through interaction between structural decay and political choice." Use non-teleology as the closing qualifier.
**Back:** *"Outcomes are built, not announced."*

### Flashcard WH-4100 — Cold War end chronology ladder (1985 to 2004)
**Front:** What is the ladder of dates for the end of the Cold War and the new order?
**Definition (learn):** Each stage is a distinct milestone or legal category; keep them separate.
**Breakdown:**
- 1985: Gorbachev (WH-4070).
- August 1988: Solidarity strikes; 1989 negotiations and elections (WH-4074).
- 9 November 1989: Berlin Wall opened (WH-4076).
- 12 September 1990: Two Plus Four signed (WH-4077).
- 3 October 1990: legal reunification (WH-4078).
- 29 November 1990: Resolution 678 (WH-4083).
- August 1991: failed coup (WH-4080).
- 25 December 1991: Gorbachev resigns (WH-4079).
- 1992–93: Maastricht, signed 1992 and in force 1993 (WH-4091).
- 1995: Dayton (WH-4087).
- 1999: Kosovo and NATO (WH-4088).
- 2004: eastern enlargement (WH-4092).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 21 §14.11 date control (✅ owner 21); Wikipedia confirmations as cited on the individual cards.
**Links:** WH-4069, WH-4101.
**Trap:** Keep the dates distinct; do not merge stages (❌ owner 21 §14.11).
**Mains hook:** Use the ladder to keep a chronology answer exact.
**Back:** *"Eighty-five to oh-four: a dozen dates, a dozen separate stories."*

### Flashcard WH-4101 — Trap bundle: the 1989–91 myths
**Front:** Which popular myths about 1989–91 does the owner reject?
**Definition (learn):** The owner lists a set of myths and their corrections.
**Breakdown:**
- "Gorbachev intended to destroy communism" → he tried to reform and save it (WH-4070).
- "Western pressure alone ended the Cold War" → structural weakness, reform, mobilisation, diplomacy (WH-4073).
- "The Wall's opening automatically reunified Germany" → Two Plus Four and 3 October 1990 (WH-4076, WH-4077).
- "Reform automatically saves rigid systems" → reform can accelerate collapse (✅ owner 21 adv §10).
- "The Gulf War was a permanent UN army" → Council-authorised coalition (WH-4084).
- "NATO's Balkan intervention proves the UN worked smoothly" → delay and improvisation (WH-4088).
- "The end of the Cold War produced stable peace" → new conflicts in new forms (✅ owner 21 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 21 §11 and adv §10.
**Links:** WH-4070, WH-4073, WH-4076, WH-4084, WH-4088.
**Trap:** These are the usual distractors in MCQs on this topic.
**Mains hook:** Use the corrections to structure the counter-argument paragraph.
**Back:** *"Not destroyed, not Western alone, not automatic, not a UN army, not a smooth peace."*

### Flashcard WH-4102 — Mains hook bundle: the new order as "unipolar but unequal"
**Front:** What is the owner's final qualified formulation for the post-1991 order?
**Definition (learn):** A unipolar moment inside a still plural and unequal international society. Real as a brief concentration of capability and Council alignment, but incomplete as an order.
**Breakdown:**
- Gulf coalition showed unusual alignment (✅ owner 21 §14.2).
- Yugoslavia exposed weak consensus over civil war and intervention (✅ owner 21 §14.2).
- Uneven globalisation and continuing postcolonial sovereignty exposed the limits of universal convergence (✅ owner 21 §14.10).
- European integration changed from containing Germany to absorbing the post-communist east (✅ owner 21 §14.10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None beyond owner.
**Links:** WH-4085, WH-4084, WH-4086, WH-4093, WH-4094.
**Trap:** Do not claim the new order was settled or entirely absent; the owner's qualification is the answer (⚠️ owner 21 §14.2).
**Mains hook:** "Did the Gulf War demonstrate a durable post-Cold-War order?" Use this formulation as the verdict.
**Back:** *"Unipolar in guns, plural in rules — an order still under negotiation."*
